3.2 '538's seat p x the model's own P(decides | tie)': what conditioning on 538's mean would do
    (a) the record: the swing given the tie from the model's own mean for the seat;
    (b) the swing given the tie from 538's mean, with the model's sd_a and s_e;
    (c) 538's mean and sd as the seat's marginal: s_e,i = sqrt(sd_538^2 - sd_a^2) (as the presidency treats 538),
        where sd_538 > 1.05 sd_a, else (b).

10.4 THE RULE AS WRITTEN (REPORT 2.6, 2.7, 0.1) AGAINST WHAT WAS APPLIED (chambers.py near_tie_test, variant_test, as backtest.json records it)
  Written: a scheme/variant is adopted only if, on the years it reaches, it (1) raises the mean log score, (2) brings the 49-51% count
  nearer the count seen, (3) moves no safe-band count further from the count seen; of those that pass, the better log score.
  Applied (reproduced above): (1) and (2) strict (>, <); (3) 'further' strict (>), so an equal distance passes; the variants'
  safe cells are tested where the count seen or none's expected is 5 or more; bands by none's mean; the near-tie test's safe
  band is 49-51% in |mean - 1/2| >= 0.10 only. Both tests pick the best log score among those that pass.
  every recorded fitting set (fit_years, k, lambda, F) holds earlier years only: yes [measured over the files and backtest.json]
  Since #51 (REPORT 0.1) a fourth condition: (4) the three hold with any one scored year left out (10.6 below).
  house: variants passing (1)-(3) none (best 'none'); passing (4) too none; the best log score among them 'none'; recorded adopted 'none' -> consistent
  senate: variants passing (1)-(3) none (best 'none'); passing (4) too none; the best log score among them 'none'; recorded adopted 'none' -> consistent
  governor: variants passing (1)-(3) ['t4', 'both'] (best 't4'); passing (4) too none; the best log score among them 'none'; recorded adopted 'none' -> consistent
